# Codex audit: Phase 5.7 V2.3 — async Transport surface

## Context

V2.3 is the third cycle of Phase 5.7 V2. V2.1 (LoopbackPair foundations,
sync) and V2.2 (full sync Transport surface) closed clean. V2.3 ships
the async portion: `Transport.websocketConnect` + `flushPendingToTransport`.

V1 megaudit Opus-B M1 + M2 pre-cleared the design decisions: napi-rs
`async fn` via the `async` (= tokio_rt) feature. `&mut self` async
methods require `unsafe` per napi-rs's discipline marker.

## Targets

**Engine repo** (`feat/quantbook-engine`, HEAD `1e2354cb7b1`):

- `Cargo.toml`: napi feature added `"async"` (pulls tokio_rt).
- `crates/ql-bindings-node/Cargo.toml`: new dep on `ql-collab-ws`.
- `crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs`:
  - New `use ql_collab_ws::WebSocketTransport;`
  - New `CollabSession::flushPendingToTransport()` — `pub async unsafe fn
    (&mut self) -> Result<()>`. Returns Promise<void> in JS.
  - New `Transport::websocketConnect(url: String) -> Result<Transport>`
    — `pub async fn`. Static factory; returns Promise<Transport>.

**IDE repo** (`feat/visualise-v1`, HEAD `6616e28a2a3`):

- `src/quantbook/types.ts`: extended `CollabSessionInstance` and
  `Transport` constructor type with the async signatures.
- `test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts`: new `quantbook V2.3 -- async
  Transport surface` suite (7 tests) + `spawnWsRelay` helper (~50 LOC
  using Node's `ws` npm package).

Tests: IDE mocha 68/68. fmt + clippy clean.

## Audit scope

1. **`&mut self async unsafe` discipline**: napi-rs requires the
   `unsafe` marker. Audit:
   - Is the safety rationale documented well enough that V2.4+ won't
     accidentally remove the `unsafe` keyword thinking it's redundant?
   - Could a misbehaving JS caller (multi-Worker scenario, even though
     V1 megaudit said the wrapper isn't `Send`) actually trigger
     concurrent `&mut` access? Verify against napi-rs's `Reference<T>`
     semantics. (V1 megaudit Opus-A MEDIUM-1 closure pinned this.)

2. **tokio runtime ownership**: napi-rs's `async` feature pulls
   `tokio_rt`. Audit:
   - Where does the tokio runtime live? (Likely a thread-local or
     LazyLock inside napi-rs.)
   - What's the runtime's worker thread count? Default tokio is N_CPUS.
   - Are there interactions between this binding's tokio runtime and
     `ql-collab-ws::WebSocketTransport`'s OWN tokio usage? They should
     share — `WebSocketTransport::connect` is `async fn` that expects
     to run on some runtime.
   - Stack/heap pressure on cold start (first async call allocates the
     runtime).

3. **`Transport.websocketConnect` rejection paths**: 3 failure
   categories (InvalidUrl, ConnectFailed, HandshakeFailed). Audit:
   - Are all three reachable from the V2.3 tests?
   - Does the error message preserve discrimination, or do they all
     collapse to the same shape via `format!("{e}")`? (V1 megaudit
     Opus M-3 + V2.2 Opus M-3 carry-forward — V2.3 introduces NEW
     async error sources.)

4. **`flushPendingToTransport` blocking concern**: the engine's
   `flush_pending_to_transport` is synchronous (Condvar wait). The
   binding's docstring claims napi-rs's async wrapper bridges this
   safely. Audit:
   - Does the docstring's claim hold? Specifically: does napi-rs run
     the body on a tokio task such that a Condvar block doesn't
     freeze V8?
   - The docstring notes V2.3 doesn't use `spawn_blocking` (it
     considered + rejected). Is this analysis correct?
   - If the engine's Condvar wait IS long-blocking, what happens to
     other concurrent JS work on the V8 thread?

5. **Test fixture safety**: `spawnWsRelay` uses Node's `ws` package
   to spawn a WebSocketServer on 127.0.0.1:0. Audit:
   - Port leakage between tests (each test should spin up a fresh
     relay; closure should be reliable).
   - Race conditions between connect + first message + close (the
     50ms setTimeout in the round-trip test is a soft sync point).
   - Does the relay correctly fan out to ALL OTHER clients excluding
     the sender? (Sender-self-receive would corrupt CRDT state.)

6. **`Transport` single-use semantics still hold post-websocketConnect**:
   the returned Transport's `isAttachable()` should be true; passing
   it to two sessions should reject the second. Test coverage?

7. **Rule 4 sweep**: any new `!Send` / `!Sync` / `!Unpin` claims in
   V2.3? Check both the napi binding source AND the async fn
   signatures.

8. **No-fallback discipline**: any silent error swallowing in the
   new async paths? Per CLAUDE.md.

## Output format

PASS / PASS-WITH-FINDINGS / FAIL. HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW/OBS, each with
location + what + why + recommended closure. Be specific.

# VS Code Copilot Instructions

## Project Overview

Visual Studio Code is built with a layered architecture using TypeScript, web APIs and Electron, combining web technologies with native app capabilities. The codebase is organized into key architectural layers:

### Root Folders
- `src/`: Main TypeScript source code with unit tests in `src/vs/*/test/` folders
- `build/`: Build scripts and CI/CD tools
- `extensions/`: Built-in extensions that ship with VS Code
- `test/`: Integration tests and test infrastructure
- `scripts/`: Development and build scripts
- `resources/`: Static resources (icons, themes, etc.)
- `out/`: Compiled JavaScript output (generated during build)

### Core Architecture (`src/` folder)
- `src/vs/base/` - Foundation utilities and cross-platform abstractions
- `src/vs/platform/` - Platform services and dependency injection infrastructure
- `src/vs/editor/` - Text editor implementation with language services, syntax highlighting, and editing features
- `src/vs/workbench/` - Main application workbench for web and desktop
  - `workbench/browser/` - Core workbench UI components (parts, layout, actions)
  - `workbench/services/` - Service implementations
  - `workbench/contrib/` - Feature contributions (git, debug, search, terminal, etc.)
  - `workbench/api/` - Extension host and VS Code API implementation
- `src/vs/code/` - Electron main process specific implementation
- `src/vs/server/` - Server specific implementation

The core architecture follows these principles:
- **Layered architecture** - from `base`, `platform`, `editor`, to `workbench`
- **Dependency injection** - Services are injected through constructor parameters
- **Contribution model** - Features contribute to registries and extension points
- **Cross-platform compatibility** - Abstractions separate platform-specific code

### Built-in Extensions (`extensions/` folder)
The `extensions/` directory contains first-party extensions that ship with VS Code:
- **Language support** - `typescript-language-features/`, `html-language-features/`, `css-language-features/`, etc.
- **Core features** - `git/`, `debug-auto-launch/`, `emmet/`, `markdown-language-features/`
- **Themes** - `theme-*` folders for default color themes
- **Development tools** - `extension-editing/`, `vscode-api-tests/`

Each extension follows the standard VS Code extension structure with `package.json`, TypeScript sources, and contribution points to extend the workbench through the Extension API.

### Finding Related Code
1. **Semantic search first**: Use file search for general concepts
2. **Grep for exact strings**: Use grep for error messages or specific function names
3. **Follow imports**: Check what files import the problematic module
4. **Check test files**: Often reveal usage patterns and expected behavior

## Validating TypeScript changes

MANDATORY: Always check the `VS Code - Build` watch task output via #runTasks/getTaskOutput for compilation errors before running ANY script or declaring work complete, then fix all compilation errors before moving forward.

- NEVER run tests if there are compilation errors
- NEVER use `npm run compile` to compile TypeScript files but call #runTasks/getTaskOutput instead

### TypeScript compilation steps
- Monitor the `VS Code - Build` task outputs for real-time compilation errors as you make changes
- This task runs `Core - Build` and `Ext - Build` to incrementally compile VS Code TypeScript sources and built-in extensions
- Start the task if it's not already running in the background

### TypeScript validation steps
- Use the run test tool if you need to run tests. If that tool is not available, then you can use `scripts/test.sh` (or `scripts\test.bat` on Windows) for unit tests (add `--grep <pattern>` to filter tests) or `scripts/test-integration.sh` (or `scripts\test-integration.bat` on Windows) for integration tests (integration tests end with .integrationTest.ts or are in /extensions/).
- Use `npm run valid-layers-check` to check for layering issues

## Coding Guidelines

### Indentation

We use tabs, not spaces.

### Naming Conventions

- Use PascalCase for `type` names
- Use PascalCase for `enum` values
- Use camelCase for `function` and `method` names
- Use camelCase for `property` names and `local variables`
- Use whole words in names when possible

### Types

- Do not export `types` or `functions` unless you need to share it across multiple components
- Do not introduce new `types` or `values` to the global namespace

### Comments

- Use JSDoc style comments for `functions`, `interfaces`, `enums`, and `classes`

### Strings

- Use "double quotes" for strings shown to the user that need to be externalized (localized)
- Use 'single quotes' otherwise
- All strings visible to the user need to be externalized using the `vs/nls` module
- Externalized strings must not use string concatenation. Use placeholders instead (`{0}`).

### UI labels
- Use title-style capitalization for command labels, buttons and menu items (each word is capitalized).
- Don't capitalize prepositions of four or fewer letters unless it's the first or last word (e.g. "in", "with", "for").

### Style

- Use arrow functions `=>` over anonymous function expressions
- Only surround arrow function parameters when necessary. For example, `(x) => x + x` is wrong but the following are correct:

```typescript
x => x + x
(x, y) => x + y
<T>(x: T, y: T) => x === y
```

- Always surround loop and conditional bodies with curly braces
- Open curly braces always go on the same line as whatever necessitates them
- Parenthesized constructs should have no surrounding whitespace. A single space follows commas, colons, and semicolons in those constructs. For example:

```typescript
for (let i = 0, n = str.length; i < 10; i++) {
    if (x < 10) {
        foo();
    }
}
function f(x: number, y: string): void { }
```

- Whenever possible, use in top-level scopes `export function x(…) {…}` instead of `export const x = (…) => {…}`. One advantage of using the `function` keyword is that the stack-trace shows a good name when debugging.

### Code Quality

- All files must include Microsoft copyright header
- Prefer `async` and `await` over `Promise` and `then` calls
- All user facing messages must be localized using the applicable localization framework (for example `nls.localize()` method)
- Don't add tests to the wrong test suite (e.g., adding to end of file instead of inside relevant suite)
- Look for existing test patterns before creating new structures
- Use `describe` and `test` consistently with existing patterns
- Prefer regex capture groups with names over numbered capture groups.
- If you create any temporary new files, scripts, or helper files for iteration, clean up these files by removing them at the end of the task
- Never duplicate imports. Always reuse existing imports if they are present.
- Do not use `any` or `unknown` as the type for variables, parameters, or return values unless absolutely necessary. If they need type annotations, they should have proper types or interfaces defined.
- When adding file watching, prefer correlated file watchers (via fileService.createWatcher) to shared ones.
- When adding tooltips to UI elements, prefer the use of IHoverService service.

   682	    /// Read the current auto-flush policy. Returns one of
   683	    /// `"disabled"` or `"onAppend"` (see `setAutoFlushPolicy`).
   684	    ///
   685	    /// **V2.2 audit closure (Opus HIGH-1, 2026-05-22)**: throws JS Error
   686	    /// instead of returning a `'unknown'` sentinel when the engine
   687	    /// reports a variant unknown to this binding (forward-compat skew
   688	    /// between engine and binding crate versions). Per CLAUDE.md
   689	    /// no-fallback rule: silent fall-through to `'unknown'` would
   690	    /// cause JS code `policy === 'onAppend'` to silently take the
   691	    /// `disabled` branch — corrupting reconnect logic. Throwing
   692	    /// surfaces the skew loudly and forces a binding upgrade.
   693	    #[napi(js_name = "autoFlushPolicy")]
   694	    pub fn auto_flush_policy(&self) -> Result<String> {
   695	        auto_flush_policy_to_string(self.inner.auto_flush_policy())

   698	    // ==========================================================
   699	    // Phase 5.7 V2.3 (2026-05-22) — async Transport surface
   700	    // ==========================================================
   701	
   702	    /// Async flush-pending — waits for the attached transport's writer
   703	    /// task to drain (level-1 local ack per V2 V4 V1 Tier K1 contract).
   704	    ///
   705	    /// Returns a JS `Promise<void>` that resolves when:
   706	    /// - the writer has completed `send` for every queued blob, OR
   707	    /// - the transport has been detached / dropped / errored.
   708	    ///
   709	    /// **Why async**: the engine's `flush_pending_to_transport` uses
   710	    /// `Condvar::wait_timeout` on a writer-progress channel. Calling it
   711	    /// from the V8 thread synchronously would block JS for the duration
   712	    /// of the wait (up to the Condvar's internal timeout). napi-rs's
   713	    /// `#[napi]` on an `async fn` bridges to a tokio task via the
   714	    /// `tokio_rt` runtime feature — the wait happens on a tokio worker
   715	    /// thread; V8 stays unblocked.
   716	    ///
   717	    /// **Failure modes**:
   718	    /// - No transport attached → returns `Ok(())` (NOT an error;
   719	    ///   matches the engine's no-op behavior).
   720	    /// - Transport error during the wait → JS Error.
   721	    ///
   722	    /// **&mut self constraint**: napi-rs supports `async fn` methods
   723	    /// with `&mut self` via the generated `Reference<T>` wrapping. The
   724	    /// engine's `flush_pending_to_transport` is itself sync (Condvar
   725	    /// blocking), so we wrap with `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` to
   726	    /// avoid blocking a tokio worker. (Without spawn_blocking, the
   727	    /// Condvar wait would tie up a worker thread; with it, the wait
   728	    /// goes to a dedicated blocking-task thread.)
   729	    ///
   730	    /// V2.3 keeps the spawn_blocking pattern simple but inefficient:
   731	    /// each call allocates. V2.3+ may pool. V3 may push the Condvar
   732	    /// itself behind an async signal (tokio::sync::Notify).
   733	    ///
   734	    /// **`unsafe` marker (napi-rs constraint, V2.3 design note)**:
   735	    /// napi-rs requires `&mut self` async methods to be marked
   736	    /// `unsafe` because the async pause-point COULD theoretically
   737	    /// allow a concurrent JS re-entry on the same instance. In
   738	    /// practice JS is single-threaded per Worker, AND napi-rs's
   739	    /// generated wrapper holds the `Reference<T>` exclusively for
   740	    /// the duration of the await. So calling this from JS is safe.
   741	    /// The `unsafe` is a Rust-side discipline marker, not a hazard
   742	    /// for the JS caller — they see `session.flushPendingToTransport()`
   743	    /// the same as any other async method. V1 megaudit Opus M1 +
   744	    /// V2.1 Send+!Sync rationale together pin: only one thread can
   745	    /// hold `&mut Reference<T>` at a time (JS event loop + napi-rs
   746	    /// callback discipline).
   747	    #[napi(js_name = "flushPendingToTransport")]
   748	    pub async unsafe fn flush_pending_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
   749	        // **&mut self async constraint (V2.3 design note)**: we can't
   750	        // easily move `&mut self` into spawn_blocking. The engine's
   751	        // `flush_pending_to_transport` is sync + non-blocking-on-the-
   752	        // CollabSession-mutex (the Condvar wait is on the writer
   753	        // task's internal channel, not on the session). For V2.3,
   754	        // call directly without spawn_blocking. The wait IS blocking
   755	        // from a tokio worker's perspective, but napi-rs's async
   756	        // entry-point runs each call on a freshly-allocated future
   757	        // that doesn't tie up napi's threadsafe-function queue.
   758	        //
   759	        // If a future audit finds that this blocks the tokio runtime
   760	        // in practice (the V1 megaudit Opus-B M2 flagged this as a
   761	        // theoretical concern), V2.4+ should restructure either:
   762	        // - Restructure `CollabSession::flush_pending_to_transport`
   763	        //   to take a tokio runtime handle, or
   764	        // - Use `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` here with a clone-able
   765	        //   handle to the session (requires engine refactor).
   766	        self.inner
   767	            .flush_pending_to_transport()
   768	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))
   769	    }
   770	}
   771	
   772	/// Phase 5.7 V2.2 (2026-05-22) — parse a JS string into
   773	/// [`CoreAutoFlushPolicy`]. JS-idiomatic camelCase + lowercase aliases
   774	/// accepted. Rejects unknown strings with a precise error.
   775	fn parse_auto_flush_policy(s: &str) -> Result<CoreAutoFlushPolicy> {
   776	    match s {
   777	        "disabled" | "Disabled" => Ok(CoreAutoFlushPolicy::Disabled),
   778	        "onAppend" | "on-append" | "OnAppend" => Ok(CoreAutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend),
   779	        other => Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   780	            "AutoFlushPolicy must be 'disabled' or 'onAppend', got {other:?}"
   781	        ))),
   782	    }
   783	}
   784	
   785	/// Phase 5.7 V2.2 (2026-05-22) — render [`CoreAutoFlushPolicy`] as a JS
   786	/// string. Uses canonical camelCase form ("disabled", "onAppend").
   787	///
   788	/// **V2.2 audit closure (Opus HIGH-1, 2026-05-22)**: returns `Result`
   789	/// and throws a JS Error when the engine reports a variant unknown to
   790	/// this binding (forward-compat skew). The prior version returned a
   791	/// `'unknown'` sentinel string — a silent fall-through that violated
   792	/// CLAUDE.md's no-fallback rule. JS code doing `policy === 'onAppend'`
   793	/// would silently miss the new variant and fall through to the
   794	/// `disabled` branch, corrupting reconnect / sync logic. Throwing is
   795	/// the correct fail-loudly path: an engine that ships a new variant
   796	/// ahead of the binding being upgraded surfaces the skew on first
   797	/// `autoFlushPolicy()` call.
   798	///
   799	/// The engine's `AutoFlushPolicy` is `#[non_exhaustive]` so V2.3+ must
   800	/// add new variants here when the engine ships them. The compile-time
   801	/// match-exhaustiveness check helps but doesn't catch new variants
   802	/// (because `#[non_exhaustive]` forces the catch-all arm).
   803	fn auto_flush_policy_to_string(p: CoreAutoFlushPolicy) -> Result<String> {
   804	    match p {
   805	        CoreAutoFlushPolicy::Disabled => Ok("disabled".to_string()),
   806	        CoreAutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend => Ok("onAppend".to_string()),
   807	        // Throw on unknown variant rather than silently returning a
   808	        // sentinel. See V2.2 audit closure note above for rationale.
   809	        other => Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   810	            "autoFlushPolicy: engine reported unknown variant {other:?} — \
   811	             this binding crate ({}) is older than the engine; upgrade \
   812	             ql-bindings-node to add the new variant's JS string mapping",
   813	            env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
   814	        ))),
   815	    }
   816	}
   817	
   818	// =============================================================
   819	// Phase 5.7 V2.1 (2026-05-22) — Transport binding
   820	// =============================================================
   821	
   822	/// JS-facing opaque wrapper for a `Box<dyn ql_collab::Transport + Send>`.
   823	///
   824	/// **Design rationale (Phase 5.7 V1 megaudit Opus-B HIGH-2 closure,
   825	/// 2026-05-22)**: napi-rs cannot bind generic functions. The engine's
   826	/// `CollabSession::attach_transport<T>` accepts any `T: Transport + Send +
   827	/// 'static`. To make this reachable from JS we:
   828	///   1. Added `CollabSession::attach_transport_boxed(Box<dyn Transport + Send>)`
   829	///      on the Rust side as a sibling entry point (V2.1 cycle).
   830	///   2. Expose this opaque `Transport` class wrapping the boxed trait
   831	///      object. JS code obtains instances from static factories
   832	///      (`Transport.loopbackPair()` here; `Transport.websocketConnect(url)`
   833	///      in V2.3) and passes them to `CollabSession.attachTransport(t)`.
   834	///
   835	/// **Single-use semantics**: an instance owns its boxed trait object.
   836	/// `CollabSession.attachTransport` MOVES the box out of this wrapper,
   837	/// leaving it consumed. Subsequent attach calls with the same wrapper
   838	/// return a JS Error ("Transport has already been consumed by attachTransport").

   840	/// doesn't have a move primitive.

   843	/// is a fat pointer to one of several impls (Loopback, WebSocket, ...).

   846	#[napi]
   847	pub struct Transport {

   861	    #[napi(js_name = "isAttachable")]
   862	    pub fn is_attachable(&self) -> bool {
   863	        self.inner.is_some()

   870	    /// Connect to a WebSocket peer and return a Transport wrapping the

   872	    ///
   873	    /// **URL format**: standard `ws://host:port` (no TLS in V2.3 — V2.4+
   874	    /// will add `wss://` once the engine's `ql-collab-ws` exposes TLS).
   875	    ///
   876	    /// **Failure modes** (all surface as JS Promise rejections):
   877	    /// - `WebSocketError::InvalidUrl` — URL parse failed.
   878	    /// - `WebSocketError::ConnectFailed` — TCP/DNS error.

   880	    ///   (e.g., HTTP 401, protocol mismatch).

   952	    // in `new()` so the shared queue exists before either end is

   955	    b: Option<LoopbackTransport>,

     1	# Quantbook Engine — Cargo workspace
     2	# Phase 0 viability spike. See ../.plans/_QUANTBOOK-v1-SPECIFICATION.md Part V.
     3	#
     4	# IMPORTANT: per Cargo's rules (https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/workspaces.html),
     5	# [workspace.dependencies] entries CANNOT carry `optional = true`. Optionality lives on the
     6	# consuming crate's Cargo.toml. There is no `[workspace.features]` table — features belong on
     7	# member crates. This file is the result of codex r9 audit corrections.
     8	
     9	[workspace]
    10	resolver = "2"
    11	members = ["crates/*"]
    12	# Phase 0 active set (other crates are stubs reserving the workspace tree):
    13	default-members = [

    15	    "crates/ql-storage",
    16	    "crates/ql-formula-syntax",
    17	    "crates/ql-formula-semantics",
    18	    "crates/ql-functions",

    26	
    27	[workspace.package]
    28	version = "0.1.0"
    29	edition = "2021"
    30	# MSRV floor 1.85: required for edition2024 (clap_lex 1.1.0 transitively via criterion). Day 2
    31	# bump from 1.83. Build is pinned to 1.95.0 in rust-toolchain.toml.
    32	rust-version = "1.85"
    33	license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0"
    34	publish = false
    35	
    36	[workspace.dependencies]
    37	# Apache Arrow — exact pins for Phase 0. All 7 crates kept in lock-step at the same patch
    38	# (58.3.0 is the latest 58.x patch as of 2026-05-11; arrow-array 58.1.0's `^58` transitive
    39	# would otherwise drift arrow-buffer to 58.3 and break the pin guard).
    40	arrow = "=58.3.0"
    41	arrow-array = "=58.3.0"
    42	arrow-buffer = "=58.3.0"
    43	arrow-schema = "=58.3.0"
    44	arrow-select = "=58.3.0"
    45	arrow-arith = "=58.3.0"
    46	arrow-cast = "=58.3.0"
    47	
    48	# Concurrency
    49	rayon = "=1.12.0"
    50	tokio = { version = "=1.50.0", default-features = false, features = ["rt-multi-thread", "macros", "fs", "net", "time", "sync"] }
    51	
    52	# Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 4 (2026-05-21) — WebSocket transport in ql-collab-ws.
    53	# tokio-tungstenite is the de-facto async websocket client for tokio.
    54	# futures-util provides SplitSink / SplitStream + StreamExt::next /
    55	# SinkExt::send. MVP scope: no TLS feature flags (plain ws:// only);
    56	# V2 V4 adds rustls. tokio-tungstenite 0.29.0 depends on tokio ^1.0
    57	# (compat with our =1.50.0 pin) and futures-util ^0.3.28.
    58	tokio-tungstenite = "=0.29.0"
    59	futures-util = { version = "=0.3.32", default-features = false, features = ["std", "sink"] }
    60	
    61	# Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 3 (2026-05-21) — Tier J3 + K5. Compile-time
    62	# `assert_impl_all!` proofs for `WebSocketTransport: Sync` and
    63	# `WebSocketError: Send + Sync`. (The V2 V3 step 4 docstring claimed
    64	# !Sync; step 3 implementation revealed the type is actually Sync —
    65	# all fields happen to be Sync; the single-consumer mpsc constraint
    66	# is enforced by `try_recv(&mut self)`, not by !Sync.)
    67	# `static_assertions` is a tiny no-runtime-cost crate exposing the

    69	static_assertions = "=1.1.0"

    71	# Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) — VS Code extension binding via napi-rs.
    72	# `ql-bindings-node` builds a `.node` cdylib loaded by the quantlab

    74	# major; napi-derive provides the `#[napi]` proc-macro; napi-build is

    78	# for V1 we load the raw cdylib via an explicit path in TS code.

     2	name = "ql-bindings-node"
     3	version.workspace = true

     9	# **Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) — VS Code extension binding.**
    10	# Previously a Phase 6+ reserved placeholder (see `ql-bindings-c` for
    11	# the parallel C-FFI placeholder, still reserved). Filled at Phase 5.7
    12	# V1 to bind `CollabSession` for the quantlab VS Code fork's extension
    13	# host (Node.js).
    14	#
    15	# cdylib output for Node.js to load. The build artifact lives at
    16	# `target/{debug,release}/libql_bindings_node.{dylib,so,dll}` per Cargo
    17	# convention. The IDE extension's loader.ts is responsible for finding
    18	# this path (env var `QUANTBOOK_ENGINE_PATH` defaulting to a workspace-
    19	# relative dev location). V2+ will publish via `@napi-rs/cli` or a
    20	# custom artifact pipeline.
    21	#
    22	# Why napi-rs (not wasm or subprocess): see `.plans/_active.md` table.
    23	# tl;dr: only option that preserves the engine's tokio runtime AND
    24	# gives direct memory access for hot paths.
    25	[lib]
    26	path = "src/lib.rs"
    27	crate-type = ["cdylib", "rlib"]
    28	
    29	[dependencies]
    30	# napi8 feature + default-features = false set at workspace level
    31	# (see workspace Cargo.toml). napi8 targets Node.js 12+ — VS Code's
    32	# electron / Node.js bundle is well past that floor.
    33	napi = { workspace = true }
    34	napi-derive = { workspace = true }
    35	
    36	# **V1 scope (per `.plans/_active.md`):** `CollabSession` only.
    37	# **V2.1 + V2.2 (2026-05-22):** added sync Transport surface
    38	# (LoopbackPair + attach/detach/flush*/poll* + AutoFlushPolicy).
    39	# **V2.3 (2026-05-22):** added `ql-collab-ws` for the
    40	# `WebSocketTransport.connect` async binding (this dep stays optional
    41	# for V2.3+ work; ql-collab core stays runtime-agnostic).
    42	ql-collab    = { path = "../ql-collab" }
    43	ql-collab-ws = { path = "../ql-collab-ws" }
    44	ql-oplog     = { path = "../ql-oplog" }
    45	ql-types     = { path = "../ql-types" }
    46	
    47	[build-dependencies]
    48	napi-build = { workspace = true }

   106		detachTransport(): boolean;

   108		/** `true` iff a transport is currently attached. */

   110	
   111		/**
   112		 * Full-snapshot flush to the attached transport. Returns `true` if
   113		 * bytes were sent, `false` if no transport is attached.
   114		 *
   115		 * **Use the upcoming `flushDeltaToTransport` instead in production**
   116		 * once V2.2 ships -- delta flushes are O(per-op delta) vs O(full
   117		 * state).
   118		 *
   119		 * @throws Error if the transport's `send` returns an error.
   120		 */
   121		flushToTransport(): boolean;
   122	
   123		/**
   124		 * Drain inbound BLOBS from the attached transport (single-pass).
   125		 * Returns the count of BLOBS drained (NOT the count of ops),
   126		 * capped by the engine's default poll limit (`DEFAULT_POLL_REMOTE_LIMIT
   127		 * = 64`). Each blob is one snapshot/delta that may contain many ops;
   128		 * to count ops, compare `opCount()` before vs after.
   129		 *
   130		 * `0` if no transport is attached or no bytes were queued.
   131		 *
   132		 * **V2.1 audit closure (Codex MEDIUM-1, 2026-05-22)**: the prior

   136		 * empirically discovered this and now pins the blob-count contract.
   137		 *
   138		 * @throws Error if the transport's `try_recv` or the merge step
   139		 *               returns an error.
   140		 */
   141		pollRemote(): number;
   142	
   143		// =====================================================================
   144		// Phase 5.7 V2.2 (2026-05-22) -- full sync Transport surface
   145		// =====================================================================
   146	
   147		/**
   148		 * Delta flush to the attached transport. Sends ONLY the ops added

   151		 *
   152		 * **Production default**. Prefer this over `flushToTransport`
   153		 * (full-snapshot) which is O(full state). Delta flushes are
   154		 * O(per-op delta).
   155		 *
   156		 * Idempotency: no state changed since last flush -> returns
   157		 * `false` without invoking `transport.send` (closes the V2 V2
   158		 * audit echo-loop concern).
   159		 *
   160		 * Per V2 V3 step 1: `attachTransport` resets the per-transport

   175		 * - Returned count `=== limit` -> more blobs may be queued; call again.

   177		 *
   178		 * @param limit Non-negative integer in `[0, u32::MAX]`. NaN /
   179		 *              Infinity / fractional / negative throw.

    46	function engineAvailable(): { ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: string } {
    47		const enginePath = resolveEnginePath();
    48		if (!fs.existsSync(enginePath)) {
    49			return {
    50				ok: false,
    51				reason: `engine binary not found at ${enginePath} -- build with: cd ../quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine && cargo build -p ql-bindings-node --release`,
    52			};

    54		return { ok: true };
    55	}
    56	
    57	function shouldSkip(): { skip: boolean; reason?: string } {
    58		const r = engineAvailable();
    59		if (!r.ok) {
    60			const seen = global as unknown as { __quantbookEngineSkipLogged?: boolean };
    61			if (!seen.__quantbookEngineSkipLogged) {
    62				seen.__quantbookEngineSkipLogged = true;
    63				console.warn(`[quantbook-roundtrip.test] SKIPPING: ${r.reason}`);
    64			}
    65			return { skip: true, reason: r.reason };
    66		}
    67		return { skip: false };
    68	}

   437			// `PeerID::MAX` as an internal sentinel (verified at
   438			// `loro-internal-1.12.0/src/loro.rs:184` --
   439			// `if peer == PeerID::MAX { return Err(...) }`). Loro returned
   440			// a clean Err so no panic-abort hazard, but added FFI-side
   441			// pre-rejection for symmetry with the peer_id==0 rejection
   442			// and to give a consistent error message.
   443			const uMax = (1n << 64n) - 1n; // u64::MAX
   444			const oneUnderUMax = uMax - 1n;
   445			const oneOverUMax = 1n << 64n;
   446			// u64::MAX is rejected (PeerID::MAX sentinel).
   447			assert.throws(() => createSession(uMax), /u64::MAX|sentinel/i,
   448				'u64::MAX rejected with sentinel-mention message');
   449			// u64::MAX - 1 IS valid -- the LARGEST acceptable peer id.
   450			const sNearMax = createSession(oneUnderUMax);
   451			assert.strictEqual(sNearMax.peerId(), oneUnderUMax,
   452				'peerId BigInt round-trips losslessly at u64::MAX - 1');
   453			// 2^64 is out of u64 range.
   454			assert.throws(() => createSession(oneOverUMax), /lossy|fit/i);
   455		});
   456	});

   512			assert.ok(!tA.isAttachable(), 'wrapper consumed by attach');
   513

   517				/already been consumed/,
   518				'second attach with consumed wrapper throws',

   232		// =====================================================================
   233		// Phase 5.7 V2.3 (2026-05-22) -- async Transport surface

   235	
   236		/**
   237		 * Async flush-pending. Waits for the attached transport's writer
   238		 * task to drain (level-1 local ack per V2 V4 V1 Tier K1).
   239		 *
   240		 * Resolves when:
   241		 * - writer has completed `send` for every queued blob, OR
   242		 * - transport has been detached / dropped / errored.
   243		 *
   244		 * **Why async**: the engine's `flush_pending_to_transport` is
   245		 * sync (Condvar wait). Calling synchronously from V8 would block
   246		 * the JS event loop until the wait completes. napi-rs's `async fn`
   247		 * bridge runs the body on a tokio worker; V8 stays unblocked.
   248		 *
   249		 * **No-op on no transport**: returns resolved Promise (NOT a
   250		 * rejection).
   251		 *
   252		 * @throws Error if the transport reports an error during the wait.
   253		 */
   254		flushPendingToTransport(): Promise<void>;
   255	}
   256	
   257	/**
   258	 * Auto-flush policy string union. Mirrors `ql_collab::AutoFlushPolicy`
   259	 * (Rust enum), bound as JS strings via the napi
   260	 * `setAutoFlushPolicy` / `autoFlushPolicy` methods.
   261	 *
   262	 * - `'disabled'`: explicit-drive (V2 V1 behavior). Caller invokes
   263	 *   `flushDeltaToTransport` + `pollRemote*` on a tick.
   264	 * - `'onAppend'`: every mutator + `pollRemote*` auto-fires a delta
   265	 *   flush.
   266	 *
   267	 * The engine's underlying enum is `#[non_exhaustive]`. Future variants
   268	 * require this union to be widened AND the napi binding's
   269	 * `parse_auto_flush_policy` + `auto_flush_policy_to_string` helpers to
   270	 * be updated. Until both layers are upgraded, `autoFlushPolicy()`
   271	 * throws on the new variant (V2.2 closure of Opus HIGH-1: no
   272	 * silent-sentinel fall-through).
   273	 */
   274	export type AutoFlushPolicy = 'disabled' | 'onAppend';

   283	// =====================================================================
   284	// Phase 5.7 V2.1 (2026-05-22) -- Transport binding type declarations
   285	// =====================================================================
   286	
   287	/**
   288	 * Opaque wrapper for a `Box<dyn ql_collab::Transport + Send>`.
   289	 *
   290	 * **Single-use semantics**: an instance owns its boxed trait object.
   291	 * `CollabSession.attachTransport(t)` MOVES the box out, leaving the
   292	 * wrapper consumed. After consumption, `isAttachable()` returns
   293	 * `false` and subsequent `attachTransport` calls with the same
   294	 * wrapper throw.
   295	 *
   296	 * Instances are obtained from factory classes (V2.1: `LoopbackPair`;
   297	 * V2.3+: `Transport.websocketConnect(url)`).
   298	 */
   299	export interface TransportInstance {
   300		/**
   301		 * `true` while this wrapper still owns its inner transport.
   302		 * `false` after passing to `attachTransport` (or any other
   303		 * future API that consumes the wrapper).
   304		 *
   305		 * Useful for branching without exception handling:
   306		 * ```ts
   307		 * if (transport.isAttachable()) {
   308		 *   session.attachTransport(transport);
   309		 * }
   310		 * ```
   311		 */
   312		isAttachable(): boolean;
   313	}
   314	
   315	/**
   316	 * Two-ended in-process Transport pair (`LoopbackTransport`).
   317	 *
   318	 * **V2.1 entry point** for obtaining paired Transport instances. The
   319	 * pair's two ends share an in-process queue: bytes sent on end A
   320	 * arrive at end B's `try_recv` and vice versa.

   666		test('flushDeltaToTransport second call with no state change short-circuits to false', () => {
   667			// V2 V3 step 1 idempotency: the SECOND flushDeltaToTransport
   668			// with no state change since the first returns Ok(false)
   669			// without invoking transport.send.
   670			//
   671			// **First flush after attach is NOT a no-op** even on an empty
   672			// log: attach resets last_flushed_vv to None, so the first
   673			// flush encodes from the empty VV and sends a baseline blob.
   674			// Subsequent flushes with no new state hit the idempotency
   675			// guard.
   676			const sessionA = createSession(1n);

   681			const first = sessionA.flushDeltaToTransport();

   683				'first delta flush after attach sends a baseline (VV reset by attach)');
   684			// Second flush with no state change: idempotency short-circuit.
   685			const second = sessionA.flushDeltaToTransport();

   711		test('flushDeltaToTransport vs flushToTransport: delta idempotency vs full unconditional send', () => {
   712			// **V2.2 audit closure (Codex LOW-3, 2026-05-22)**: comment
   713			// re-grounded. Prior version said "delta will send 0 ops" which
   714			// was misleading — the first delta after attach DOES send the
   715			// pending op (from empty VV) and advances last_flushed_vv. The
   716			// second delta with no state change is the no-op. The
   717			// behavioral comparison this test pins:
   718			//   - flushDeltaToTransport: idempotency-guarded. Sends pending
   719			//     ops on first call after a state change; returns false on
   720			//     the second call with no state change.
   721			//   - flushToTransport: unconditional. Sends the full snapshot
   722			//     every call regardless of state.
   723			// Loro dedupe ensures B sees the single op once regardless of
   724			// how many times the blob arrives.
   725			const sessionA = createSession(1n);
   726			const sessionB = createSession(2n);
   727			const [tA, tB] = loopbackTransportPair();
   728			sessionA.attachTransport(tA);
   729			sessionB.attachTransport(tB);
   730	
   731			sessionA.appendPutValue(0, 0, 0, 1);
   732			assert.strictEqual(sessionA.flushDeltaToTransport(), true);
   733			// Subsequent flush with no state change is no-op.
   734			assert.strictEqual(sessionA.flushDeltaToTransport(), false,
   735				'second delta flush with no new state is no-op');
   736			// Full flush still sends (no idempotency guard on the
   737			// full-snapshot path).
   738			assert.strictEqual(sessionA.flushToTransport(), true,
   739				'full flush sends regardless of state change');
   740			sessionB.pollRemote();
   741			sessionB.pollRemote(); // drain both blobs
   742			assert.strictEqual(sessionB.opCount(), 1, 'B has the single op (deduped)');
   743		});

    16	 *    doesn't (e.g., reject negative `sheet` integers before the BigInt
    17	 *    boundary makes the error less actionable).

    19	 *    (e.g., add async retry wrappers around `mergeBytes`).

    35	/**
    36	 * Construct a fresh `CollabSession` for the given peer.

    39	 *               negative, or doesn't fit in u64.
    40	 */
    41	export function createSession(peerId: bigint): CollabSessionInstance {
    42		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    43		return new engine.CollabSession(peerId);
    44	}
    45	
    46	/**

    49	 * BEFORE the Rust code sees the value).

    52	 * `napi_get_value_uint32` applies ECMAScript ToUint32 to JS Number
    53	 * arguments. That converts:
    54	 *   - `-1` to `0xFFFFFFFF` (silent wrap)
    55	 *   - `NaN` to `0` (silent coercion)
    56	 *   - `Infinity` to `0` (silent coercion)
    57	 *   - `2.5` to `2` (silent floor-toward-zero)
    58	 *
    59	 * The Rust binding sees the post-ToUint32 value and has no way to
    60	 * detect the original JS-side intent. Validate at the TS layer
    61	 * BEFORE the call so each failure surfaces a precise error.
    62	 *
    63	 * @throws Error if sheet/row/col aren't non-negative integers in range,
    64	 *               or if value isn't a finite number.
    65	 */
    66	export function appendPutValueValidated(
    67		session: CollabSessionInstance,
    68		sheet: number,
    69		row: number,
    70		col: number,
    71		value: number,
    72	): void {
    73		if (!Number.isInteger(sheet) || sheet < 0 || sheet > 0xFFFF) {
    74			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: sheet must be an integer in [0, 65535], got ${sheet}`);
    75		}
    76		if (!Number.isInteger(row) || row < 0 || row > 0xFFFFFFFF) {
    77			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: row must be an integer in [0, 4294967295], got ${row}`);
    78		}
    79		if (!Number.isInteger(col) || col < 0 || col > 0xFFFFFFFF) {
    80			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: col must be an integer in [0, 4294967295], got ${col}`);
    81		}
    82		if (typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value)) {
    83			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: value must be a finite number, got ${value}`);
    84		}
    85		session.appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value);
    86	}

    93	 *               author new ops under this id).

    95	 */
    96	export function sessionFromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array): CollabSessionInstance {
    97		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    98		return engine.CollabSession.fromSnapshot(peerId, bytes);
    99	}

   113	/**
   114	 * Construct a fresh `LoopbackPair` and immediately take both ends.
   115	 * Returns the two Transport instances ready to attach to a session
   116	 * each.
   117	 *
   118	 * Combines `new LoopbackPair()` + `takeA()` + `takeB()` into a single
   119	 * call because the typical use case wants both ends. For cases where
   120	 * one end is created independently (e.g., delayed peer connection),
   121	 * use `createLoopbackPair()` instead and call `takeA` / `takeB`
   122	 * separately.
   123	 *
   124	 * @returns `[transportA, transportB]` where bytes sent on A arrive
   125	 *           at B's `pollRemote` and vice versa.
   126	 *
   127	 * @example
   128	 * ```ts
   129	 * const [tA, tB] = loopbackTransportPair();
   130	 * sessionA.attachTransport(tA);
   131	 * sessionB.attachTransport(tB);
   132	 * // mutate sessionA, flushToTransport, pollRemote on sessionB
   133	 * ```
   134	 */
   135	export function loopbackTransportPair(): [TransportInstance, TransportInstance] {
   136		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
   137		const pair = new engine.LoopbackPair();
   138		return [pair.takeA(), pair.takeB()];
   139	}
   140	
   141	/**
   142	 * Construct an empty `LoopbackPair` without taking either end. Use
   143	 * when the two takes need to happen at different times.
   144	 */
   145	export function createLoopbackPair(): LoopbackPairInstance {
   146		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
   147		return new engine.LoopbackPair();
   148	}

   150	// =====================================================================
   151	// Phase 5.7 V2.2 (2026-05-22) -- AutoFlushPolicy helpers
   152	// =====================================================================
   153	
   154	/**
   155	 * Type guard for the camelCase string union `AutoFlushPolicy`. Returns
   156	 * `true` for `'disabled'` or `'onAppend'`. Note: the engine's
   157	 * `setAutoFlushPolicy` also accepts variant aliases (`'Disabled'`,
   158	 * `'OnAppend'`, `'on-append'`) -- this guard is the STRICT camelCase
   159	 * check for IDE-side validation; the engine is the loose parser.
   160	 */
   161	export function isAutoFlushPolicy(value: unknown): value is AutoFlushPolicy {
   162		return value === 'disabled' || value === 'onAppend';
   163	}

   936			// bytes") with a tautological assertion `merged >= 0` — wrong

   941			// the two endpoints share their queues via Arc<Mutex<VecDeque>>.
   942			// A's outbox IS b.inbox; when A is dropped, its Arc clones
   943			// decrement but B's Arc keeps the queue ALIVE with bytes intact.
   944			// So B's pollRemote still drains A's queued bytes after A
   945			// detaches.

   982	
   983	/**
   984	 * Spawn an in-process WebSocket echo-fanout server on a random port.
   985	 *
   986	 * **V2.3 design note**: the engine's tokio-tungstenite client speaks
   987	 * the standard WebSocket binary-frame protocol. Node's `ws` package
   988	 * on the same protocol is sufficient for round-trip tests. Each
   989	 * inbound binary message is fanned out to ALL other connected
   990	 * clients — that's the minimum semantics for "two peers connected
   991	 * to a localhost relay can sync via the engine's WebSocketTransport".
   992	 */
   993	async function spawnWsRelay(): Promise<SpawnedWsServer> {
   994		return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
   995			const wss = new WebSocketServer({ port: 0, host: '127.0.0.1' });
   996			const sockets: WsClient[] = [];
   997			wss.on('connection', (ws: WsClient) => {
   998				sockets.push(ws);
   999				ws.on('message', (data: Buffer, isBinary: boolean) => {
  1000					if (!isBinary) {
  1001						return; // V2.3 only handles binary frames
  1002					}
  1003					for (const peer of sockets) {
  1004						if (peer !== ws && peer.readyState === peer.OPEN) {
  1005							peer.send(data, { binary: true });
  1006						}
  1007					}
  1008				});
  1009				ws.on('close', () => {
  1010					const i = sockets.indexOf(ws);
  1011					if (i >= 0) {
  1012						sockets.splice(i, 1);
  1013					}
  1014				});
  1015			});
  1016			wss.on('error', reject);
  1017			wss.on('listening', () => {
  1018				const addr = wss.address() as AddressInfo;
  1019				resolve({
  1020					url: `ws://127.0.0.1:${addr.port}`,
  1021					close: () => new Promise<void>((res) => {
  1022						for (const s of sockets) {
  1023							try { s.terminate(); } catch { /* ignore */ }
  1024						}
  1025						wss.close(() => res());
  1026					}),
  1027				});
  1028			});
  1029		});
  1030	}

+ * on the same protocol is sufficient for round-trip tests. Each
+ * inbound binary message is fanned out to ALL other connected
+ * clients — that's the minimum semantics for "two peers connected
+ * to a localhost relay can sync via the engine's WebSocketTransport".
+ */

+suite('quantbook V2.3 -- async Transport surface (WebSocketTransport + flushPending)', function () {
+	suiteSetup(function () {
+		const r = shouldSkip();
+		if (r.skip) {
+			this.skip();
+		}
+		_resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests();
+		this.timeout(60000);
+		loadQuantbookEngine();
+	});
+
+	test('Transport.websocketConnect rejects on invalid URL', async () => {
+		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
+		await assert.rejects(
+			engine.Transport.websocketConnect('not-a-ws-url'),
+			/invalid WebSocket URL|WebSocket connection failed|WebSocket handshake failed/,
+			'invalid URL rejects with a WebSocket-flavored error',
+		);
+	});
+
+	test('Transport.websocketConnect rejects on connection refused', async function () {
+		this.timeout(10000);
+		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
+		await assert.rejects(
+			// Port 1 is privileged + almost certainly not listening.
+			engine.Transport.websocketConnect('ws://127.0.0.1:1'),
+			/WebSocket connection failed|connection refused|WebSocket handshake failed/i,
+		);
+	});
+
+	test('Transport.websocketConnect succeeds against a localhost relay + returns attachable Transport', async function () {
+		this.timeout(15000);
+		const relay = await spawnWsRelay();
+		try {
+			const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
+			const t = await engine.Transport.websocketConnect(relay.url);
+			assert.ok(t.isAttachable(),
+				'returned Transport is attachable (single-use, fresh)');
+		} finally {
+			await relay.close();
+		}
+	});
+
+	test('two-peer round-trip via WebSocketTransport relay (V2.3 end-to-end async sync)', async function () {
+		this.timeout(20000);
+		const relay = await spawnWsRelay();
+		try {
+			const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
+			const tA = await engine.Transport.websocketConnect(relay.url);
+			const tB = await engine.Transport.websocketConnect(relay.url);
+
+			const sessionA = createSession(1n);
+			const sessionB = createSession(2n);
+			sessionA.attachTransport(tA);
+			sessionB.attachTransport(tB);
+
+			sessionA.appendPutValue(0, 0, 0, 42);
+			assert.strictEqual(sessionA.flushDeltaToTransport(), true,
+				'A delta flush queues bytes to its WebSocket writer');
+			// V2.3 closure verification: wait for the writer to actually
+			// send so the relay has the bytes to fan out.
+			await sessionA.flushPendingToTransport();
+
+			// Allow the relay to fan out + B's reader to enqueue bytes.
+			// 50ms is generous for localhost.
+			await new Promise<void>((res) => setTimeout(res, 50));
+
+			const blobsDrained = sessionB.pollRemote();
+			assert.ok(blobsDrained >= 1, `B drained at least 1 blob, got ${blobsDrained}`);
+			assert.strictEqual(sessionB.opCount(), 1,
+				'B has A op after async round-trip');
+
+			sessionA.detachTransport();
+			sessionB.detachTransport();
+		} finally {
+			await relay.close();
+		}
+	});
+
+	test('flushPendingToTransport on no-transport session resolves without error', async () => {
+		// V2.3 contract: no transport -> Ok(()) (matches engine's no-op).
+		// NOT a rejected promise.
+		const session = createSession(1n);
+		await session.flushPendingToTransport();
+	});
+
+	test('flushPendingToTransport on attached-no-pending session resolves quickly', async function () {
+		this.timeout(5000);
+		const session = createSession(1n);
+		const [tA, _tB] = loopbackTransportPair();
+		session.attachTransport(tA);
+		// No ops appended. The first flushDelta would still send a baseline
+		// blob, but flushPending without a prior flush should resolve
+		// quickly (writer has nothing queued).
+		const start = Date.now();
+		await session.flushPendingToTransport();
+		const elapsed = Date.now() - start;
+		assert.ok(elapsed < 1000,
+			`flushPending on idle session resolved in ${elapsed}ms (< 1000)`);
+	});
+
+	test('flushPending after a flushDelta drains the local writer queue', async function () {
+		this.timeout(15000);
+		const relay = await spawnWsRelay();
+		try {
+			const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
+			const tA = await engine.Transport.websocketConnect(relay.url);
+			const session = createSession(1n);
+			session.attachTransport(tA);
+
+			session.appendPutValue(0, 0, 0, 1);
+			assert.strictEqual(session.flushDeltaToTransport(), true);
+			// V2 V4 V1 Tier K1: after flushPending, the writer task has
+			// completed its send for every queued blob.
+			await session.flushPendingToTransport();
+			session.detachTransport();
+		} finally {
+			await relay.close();
+		}
+	});
+});

codex
The new binding doc currently says two different things about `spawn_blocking`: the high-level doc says it uses it, but the actual implementation comment says it does not. I’m tracing the engine wait path and napi-rs async lowering now, because that affects both correctness and how severe that documentation mismatch is.

   832	    /// this proxy, `has_pending_flush() == false` could be observed
   833	    /// while bytes have not actually been written. Calling
   834	    /// `flush_pending_to_transport()` AFTER `flush_delta_to_transport`
   835	    /// (or any mutator under `OnAppend`) closes that gap — Ok return
   836	    /// confirms the local writer task has caught up, providing a
   837	    /// level-1 ack (bytes hit the underlying transport's wire).
   838	    ///
   839	    /// # Errors
   840	    /// - `Err(CollabSessionError::Transport(Closed))` if the transport
   841	    ///   closed before completing the drain (e.g., writer task failed
   842	    ///   mid-flush; reconnect via `detach_transport` + new
   843	    ///   `attach_transport` will redeliver the bytes via the V2 V3
   844	    ///   step 1 baseline-reset contract).

   854	    /// `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` (any runtime).

   192	/// Errors emitted by [`WebSocketTransport`].
   193	///
   194	/// Distinguishes connect-time errors (`InvalidUrl`, `ConnectFailed`,
   195	/// `HandshakeFailed`) from runtime errors observed by the background
   196	/// reader/writer tasks (`RuntimeError`). Connect-time errors surface
   197	/// as the `Result::Err` from [`WebSocketTransport::connect`]; runtime
   198	/// errors are stashed on the transport and readable via
   199	/// [`WebSocketTransport::last_error`] AFTER the trait `send`/`try_recv`
   200	/// surface `TransportError::Closed`.
   201	///
   202	/// Maps tungstenite errors opaquely — we don't want to leak
   203	/// tungstenite types into the public API.
   204	///
   205	/// **V2 V3 step 4 audit closure (Opus M1, 2026-05-21):** the prior
   206	/// ship discarded background-task errors (`if let Err(_e)`), flattening
   207	/// 8 distinct tungstenite::Error variants into a uniform `Closed` at
   208	/// the trait boundary. This violated the CLAUDE.md no-fallbacks rule
   209	/// (errors must be visible) and made it impossible for IDE consumers
   210	/// driving reconnect handshakes to distinguish "connection lost" from
   211	/// "auth failed". Adds `RuntimeError(String)` variant + `last_error()`
   212	/// accessor; both tasks now populate the shared `last_error` slot on
   213	/// non-graceful exit.
   214	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Error)]
   215	#[non_exhaustive]
   216	pub enum WebSocketError {
   217	    /// The URL string failed to parse or is missing required parts.
   218	    /// Per CLAUDE.md no-fallback rule: surface clearly, don't silently
   219	    /// retry with a "fixed up" URL.
   220	    #[error("invalid WebSocket URL: {0}")]
   221	    InvalidUrl(String),
   222	
   223	    /// TCP connect failed (refused, timeout, DNS, etc.).
   224	    #[error("WebSocket connection failed: {0}")]
   225	    ConnectFailed(String),
   226	
   227	    /// WebSocket protocol handshake failed (HTTP upgrade rejected,
   228	    /// invalid response, etc.).
   229	    #[error("WebSocket handshake failed: {0}")]
   230	    HandshakeFailed(String),
   231	
   232	    /// Background reader/writer task observed a runtime failure
   233	    /// (peer reset, IO error, protocol violation, capacity exceeded,
   234	    /// etc.) AFTER a successful connect. Surfaces via
   235	    /// [`WebSocketTransport::last_error`] once the trait-level
   236	    /// `Closed` has been observed.
   237	    #[error("WebSocket runtime error: {0}")]
   238	    RuntimeError(String),
   239	}
   240	
   241	/// Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 4 — WebSocket `Transport` impl.
   242	///
   243	/// Bridges async tokio-tungstenite to the sync `Transport` trait via
   244	/// `tokio::sync::mpsc` channels and two spawned background tasks.
   245	/// See module-level docs for architecture diagram.
   246	///
   247	/// ## Construction
   248	///
   249	/// Use [`connect`](Self::connect) — async fn that performs the WS
   250	/// handshake. Caller pattern from sync code:
   251	///
   252	/// ```ignore
   253	/// let rt = tokio::runtime::Runtime::new()?;
   254	/// let ws = rt.block_on(WebSocketTransport::connect("ws://host:port"))?;
   255	/// session.attach_transport(ws);
   256	/// ```
   257	///
   258	/// ## Send + Sync
   259	///
   260	/// `WebSocketTransport: Send + Sync` (Send pinned by the V2 V3
   261	/// step 4 manual `const _ASSERT_WEBSOCKET_TRANSPORT_SEND`, Sync
   262	/// pinned by V2 V4 V1 step 3 `static_assertions::assert_impl_all!` —
   263	/// both compile-time checks at the end of this file). Note: the
   264	/// prior
   265	/// V2 V3 step 4 docstring (and Tier J3 backlog entry) incorrectly
   266	/// claimed `!Sync` based on the intuition that the single-consumer
   267	/// mpsc receiver should prevent sharing. In Rust, `Sync` means "`&T`
   268	/// can be sent across threads" — orthogonal to "`&mut T` access is
   269	/// exclusive." `try_recv` takes `&mut self`, so concurrent receives
   270	/// are already prevented by the borrow checker. All fields
   271	/// (mpsc handles, `Arc<AtomicBool>`, `Arc<Mutex<_>>`, `JoinHandle`)
   272	/// are `Send + Sync`, making the composite `Send + Sync`.

   274	/// **Why this matters**: matches the `CollabSession::transport:
   275	/// Box<dyn Transport + Send>` constraint (only `Send` is required at
   276	/// the trait boundary; `Sync` is a bonus that allows future
   277	/// `Arc<WebSocketTransport>` wrappers if a consumer needs them).
   278	/// **Tier J3 closure (V2 V4 V1 step 3, 2026-05-21):** corrected the
   279	/// docstring + the backlog entry; pinned BOTH bounds with positive
   280	/// compile-time asserts.
   281	pub struct WebSocketTransport {
   282	    /// Caller-side sender feeding the writer task. Sync write via
   283	    /// `UnboundedSender::send` (which is non-blocking).
   284	    outbound_tx: mpsc::UnboundedSender<Vec<u8>>,
   285	    /// Caller-side receiver pulling from the reader task. Sync drain
   286	    /// via `UnboundedReceiver::try_recv`.
   287	    inbound_rx: mpsc::UnboundedReceiver<Vec<u8>>,
   288	    /// Set true when either task observes a permanent failure (WS
   289	    /// close, IO error, peer disconnect) OR caller invokes
   290	    /// [`close`](Self::close). Both `send` and `try_recv` honor this
   291	    /// flag per the `Transport` trait drain-before-close contract.
   292	    closed: Arc<AtomicBool>,
   293	    /// **V2 V3 step 4 audit closure (Opus M1, 2026-05-21):** stash
   294	    /// the most recent task-observed runtime error so callers can
   295	    /// distinguish "peer reset" from "auth rejected" from "capacity
   296	    /// exceeded" etc. Populated by the writer task on `ws_sink.send`
   297	    /// error and the reader task on stream `Err`. Stays `None` for
   298	    /// clean shutdowns (caller `close()`, Drop, or graceful Close
   299	    /// frame from peer).
   300	    last_error: Arc<Mutex<Option<WebSocketError>>>,
   301	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**
   302	    /// Incremented by [`Transport::send`] on Ok return. Total bytes
   303	    /// queued lifetime (monotonic, never resets). Compared against
   304	    /// [`Self::progress.0`] in [`Transport::flush_pending`] to detect
   305	    /// when the writer task has caught up.
   306	    queued_count: Arc<AtomicU64>,
   307	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**
   308	    /// `(counter, condvar)` pair. Counter is incremented by the writer
   309	    /// task AFTER each successful `ws_sink.send`. Condvar is notified
   310	    /// on each counter increment AND on `closed`-flag transition (so
   311	    /// `flush_pending` callers waiting on the Condvar wake up and
   312	    /// observe the closed state). Sync primitives (not tokio) because
   313	    /// `flush_pending` is called from sync caller code and needs to
   314	    /// block synchronously without a tokio runtime context.
   315	    progress: Arc<(Mutex<u64>, Condvar)>,
   316	    /// Background tokio task that drains `outbound_rx` and writes
   317	    /// `Message::Binary` frames to the WebSocket sink. Aborted on
   318	    /// `Drop`.
   319	    writer_task: JoinHandle<()>,
   320	    /// Background tokio task that polls the WebSocket stream and
   321	    /// pushes binary payloads into `inbound_tx`. Aborted on `Drop`.
   322	    reader_task: JoinHandle<()>,
   323	}
   324	
   325	impl std::fmt::Debug for WebSocketTransport {
   326	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   327	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 4 (Tier K6, 2026-05-21):** surface
   328	        // last_error presence in Debug output. When a transport is
   329	        // closed and both tasks have exited, knowing whether the
   330	        // error slot is populated matters more than knowing the
   331	        // booleans alone — it distinguishes "clean caller-close"
   332	        // (last_error=None) from "task observed a runtime failure"
   333	        // (last_error=Some). Per V2 V3 step 5 megaudit Opus-B L2.
   334	        f.debug_struct("WebSocketTransport")
   335	            .field("closed", &self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   336	            .field("writer_finished", &self.writer_task.is_finished())
   337	            .field("reader_finished", &self.reader_task.is_finished())
   338	            .field("last_error_present", &self.last_error().is_some())
   339	            .finish()
   340	    }
   341	}
   342	
   343	impl WebSocketTransport {
   344	    /// Connect to a WebSocket server at `url` (e.g. `ws://host:port/path`).
   345	    ///
   346	    /// Must be called from within a tokio runtime context (the two
   347	    /// background tasks are spawned via `tokio::spawn`). The typical
   348	    /// pattern from sync code is `runtime.block_on(connect(...))`.
   349	    ///
   350	    /// On success, returns a fully-wired transport: send/recv channels
   351	    /// connected, background tasks running. On failure, returns one
   352	    /// of the [`WebSocketError`] variants with the underlying cause.
   353	    pub async fn connect(url: &str) -> Result<Self, WebSocketError> {
   354	        // tokio-tungstenite::connect_async handles URL parse + TCP
   355	        // connect + WS handshake in one call. Distinguish the failure
   356	        // modes via the returned error string match — opaque to keep
   357	        // tungstenite types out of our public API.
   358	        let (ws_stream, _response) = tokio_tungstenite::connect_async(url).await.map_err(|e| {
   359	            let msg = e.to_string();
   360	            // Coarse classification — tungstenite::Error doesn't have a
   361	            // clean discriminant for "URL parse" vs "TCP connect" vs
   362	            // "handshake". Use the error type to route.
   363	            match e {
   364	                tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Error::Url(_) => WebSocketError::InvalidUrl(msg),
   365	                tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Error::Io(_) => WebSocketError::ConnectFailed(msg),
   366	                tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Error::Http(_)
   367	                | tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Error::HttpFormat(_) => {
   368	                    WebSocketError::HandshakeFailed(msg)
   369	                }
   370	                _ => WebSocketError::HandshakeFailed(msg),
   371	            }
   372	        })?;
   373	
   374	        let (mut ws_sink, mut ws_stream) = ws_stream.split();
   375	
   376	        let (outbound_tx, mut outbound_rx) = mpsc::unbounded_channel::<Vec<u8>>();
   377	        let (inbound_tx, inbound_rx) = mpsc::unbounded_channel::<Vec<u8>>();
   378	
   379	        let closed = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
   380	        let closed_writer = Arc::clone(&closed);

   496	                        // (e.g., "session expired, please reauth").
   497	                        if let Some(cf) = frame {
   498	                            if !cf.reason.is_empty() || u16::from(cf.code) != 1000 {
   499	                                record_runtime_error(
   500	                                    &last_error_reader,
   501	                                    format!(
   502	                                        "peer close frame: code={}, reason={}",
   503	                                        u16::from(cf.code),

   505	                                    ),
   506	                                );

   510	                        progress_reader.1.notify_all();
   511	                        _guard.mark_clean_exit();

   515	                        Message::Text(_) | Message::Ping(_) | Message::Pong(_) | Message::Frame(_),

   518	                        // contract (see module-level V1 limits).

   522	                        // the tungstenite error so callers can
   523	                        // distinguish runtime causes via last_error().

   525	                        closed_reader.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   526	                        progress_reader.1.notify_all();
   527	                        _guard.mark_clean_exit();

   533	            // 2026-05-21):** stream ended without a Close frame.

   535	            // stays None) and from explicit Close frame (last_error
   536	            // populated above if frame had reason or non-1000 code).
   537	            record_runtime_error(
   538	                &last_error_reader,
   539	                "peer stream ended without close frame".to_string(),
   540	            );

   542	            progress_reader.1.notify_all();
   543	            _guard.mark_clean_exit();
   544	        });

   546	        Ok(Self {
   547	            outbound_tx,
   548	            inbound_rx,
   549	            closed,
   550	            last_error,
   551	            queued_count,
   552	            progress,
   553	            writer_task,
   554	            reader_task,
   555	        })
   556	    }

   558	    /// True if this transport has been closed — either explicitly via
   559	    /// [`close`](Self::close), via Drop, or by either background task
   560	    /// observing a permanent failure (peer disconnect, WS Close frame,
   561	    /// IO error).
   562	    pub fn is_closed(&self) -> bool {
   563	        self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   564	    }
   565	
   566	    /// **V2 V3 step 4 audit closure (Opus M1, 2026-05-21):** read the
   567	    /// most recent task-observed runtime error, if any. Returns `None`
   568	    /// for clean shutdowns (caller [`close`](Self::close), Drop, or
   569	    /// graceful Close frame from peer); returns `Some(RuntimeError(_))`
   570	    /// when the writer's `ws_sink.send` or the reader's `ws_stream.next`

   572	    ///
   573	    /// Read AFTER observing [`TransportError::Closed`] from
   574	    /// [`Transport::send`] or [`Transport::try_recv`] to distinguish
   575	    /// "peer reset" vs "auth rejected" vs "capacity exceeded" etc.

   577	    /// the right user-facing message and backoff strategy.
   578	    ///
   579	    /// Returns a clone (cheap — `WebSocketError` is small + `Clone`).
   580	    ///
   581	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-B M3, 2026-05-21):**
   582	    /// poisoned mutex no longer silently returns `None`. Recovers
   583	    /// the inner via `PoisonError::into_inner()` so the slot's
   584	    /// most-recent contents stay readable across the poison boundary.

   587	            Ok(guard) => guard.clone(),
   588	            Err(poisoned) => poisoned.into_inner().clone(),

   592	    /// Explicitly mark this transport closed. Subsequent
   593	    /// [`Transport::send`] calls return `Err(Closed)`; subsequent
   594	    /// [`Transport::try_recv`] calls drain the inbound queue first,
   595	    /// then return `Err(Closed)` (per trait contract).
   596	    ///
   597	    /// Does NOT abort the background tasks — only Drop does that. If
   598	    /// the caller wants the tasks gone, drop the transport.
   599	    ///
   600	    /// **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (Tier K1):** also notifies the
   601	    /// flush_pending Condvar so any concurrent `flush_pending` waiter
   602	    /// wakes up and observes the new closed state.
   603	    pub fn close(&self) {
   604	        self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   605	        self.progress.1.notify_all();
   606	    }

   609	impl Drop for WebSocketTransport {
   610	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   611	        // **V2 V3 step 4 audit closure (Opus M2, 2026-05-21):** the
   612	        // sole shutdown path under current design is `abort()` on both
   613	        // task handles. The graceful `outbound_rx.recv() -> None ->
   614	        // ws_sink.close()` branch in the writer task is UNREACHABLE
   615	        // FROM DROP because `Drop::drop` runs before the struct's
   616	        // fields drop (Rust drop order: body first, then fields in
   617	        // decl order). So `outbound_tx` is still alive when we call
   618	        // `writer_task.abort()`. The graceful arm only fires if a
   619	        // future "soft close" API explicitly detaches `outbound_tx`
   620	        // before drop — not part of the current MVP.
   621	        //
   622	        // `closed = true` ordering: set first so any concurrent
   623	        // sync `send`/`try_recv` observes Closed via the flag check
   624	        // before the task abort propagates.
   625	        //
   626	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (Tier K1):** also notify the
   627	        // flush_pending Condvar — a caller awaiting a long-running
   628	        // flush would otherwise wait until the next wait_timeout
   629	        // tick to observe the closed flag. (Unreachable in practice
   630	        // because Drop is called on a unique `&mut self` so no
   631	        // concurrent flush_pending can be in flight, but kept for
   632	        // safety + future shared-reference APIs.)
   633	        self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   634	        self.progress.1.notify_all();
   635	        self.writer_task.abort();
   636	        self.reader_task.abort();
   637	    }
   638	}

   640	impl Transport for WebSocketTransport {
   641	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   642	        // **LOAD-BEARING** (V2 V3 step 4 audit closure, Opus M4): this
   643	        // closed-flag check MUST precede `outbound_tx.send`. The
   644	        // `collab_session_websocket_send_after_close_surfaces_closed`
   645	        // integration test depends on observing `Closed` here (the
   646	        // reader task sets `closed = true` on peer disconnect; the
   647	        // writer task may still be parked on `outbound_rx.recv` and
   648	        // hasn't yet seen the broken sink). If a future refactor
   649	        // reorders this check (e.g., to enable an outbox-queuing-
   650	        // while-closed feature), that test will flake.
   651	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   652	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   653	        }
   654	        // UnboundedSender::send returns Err iff the receiver dropped.
   655	        // The receiver lives in the writer task; if it dropped, the
   656	        // task exited (panic, abort, or graceful). Treat as Closed.
   657	        self.outbound_tx
   658	            .send(bytes.to_vec())
   659	            .map_err(|_| TransportError::Closed)?;
   660	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**
   661	        // Bump the queued-count counter AFTER successful mpsc enqueue.
   662	        // flush_pending() reads this as the target the writer task
   663	        // must catch up to.
   664	        self.queued_count.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
   665	        Ok(())
   666	    }
   667	
   668	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
   669	        // Drain-before-close per `Transport` trait contract
   670	        // (transport.rs:112-115): "Closed only when channel
   671	        // permanently closed AND internal queue is empty."
   672	        use mpsc::error::TryRecvError;
   673	        match self.inbound_rx.try_recv() {
   674	            Ok(bytes) => Ok(Some(bytes)),
   675	            Err(TryRecvError::Empty) => {
   676	                if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   677	                    Err(TransportError::Closed)
   678	                } else {
   679	                    Ok(None)
   680	                }
   681	            }
   682	            Err(TryRecvError::Disconnected) => {
   683	                // Reader task exited and dropped inbound_tx. Mark
   684	                // closed so subsequent calls fast-path; treat as
   685	                // Closed for this call (the queue is empty AND the
   686	                // upstream is permanently gone).
   687	                self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   688	                Err(TransportError::Closed)
   689	            }
   690	        }
   691	    }
   692	
   693	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-A H1, 2026-05-21):**
   694	    /// override the default `None` to expose the runtime-error cause
   695	    /// through the trait so [`ql_collab::CollabSession::transport_last_error`]
   696	    /// can reach it after `attach_transport` consumes the concrete
   697	    /// type into `Box<dyn Transport + Send>`.
   698	    ///
   699	    /// Returns the underlying [`WebSocketError`]'s `Display` string.
   700	    /// Consumers wanting to discriminate handshake/connect/runtime
   701	    /// reasons can pattern-match on substrings or use the prefix
   702	    /// (`"WebSocket runtime error: ..."`) to detect the variant —
   703	    /// for stronger typing keep a parallel reference to the concrete
   704	    /// transport before attach.
   705	    fn last_error(&self) -> Option<String> {
   706	        Self::last_error(self).map(|e| e.to_string())
   707	    }
   708	
   709	    /// **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**
   710	    /// Block until every byte blob previously queued via `send` has
   711	    /// been written to the WebSocket sink. Closes the V2 V3 step 5
   712	    /// megaudit's convergent finding (queued-vs-acked semantics).
   713	    ///
   714	    /// Mechanism: reads `queued_count` as the target; locks the
   715	    /// `progress.0` counter and uses `Condvar::wait_timeout` to wake
   716	    /// every 100ms (so closed-flag transitions are observed promptly).
   717	    /// The writer task increments `progress.0` + `notify_all` after
   718	    /// each successful `ws_sink.send`, AND `notify_all` on closed-
   719	    /// flag transitions.
   720	    ///
   721	    /// # Errors
   722	    /// - `TransportError::Closed` if the transport was already closed
   723	    ///   on entry, OR if the writer task sets closed mid-wait (e.g.,
   724	    ///   ws_sink.send failed).
   725	    /// - `TransportError::Io` if the progress mutex is poisoned by a
   726	    ///   panicked writer task. The poisoned-mutex recovery via
   727	    ///   `PoisonError::into_inner()` is intentionally NOT used here:
   728	    ///   if the writer panicked mid-counter-increment, the counter
   729	    ///   may be in an inconsistent state (e.g., notify_all happened
   730	    ///   before counter increment); reading + retrying could
   731	    ///   busy-loop forever. Surface as Io and let the caller
   732	    ///   recover via reconnect.
   733	    fn flush_pending(&mut self) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   734	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 1 audit closure (Codex M1, 2026-05-21):**
   735	        // honor the documented contract — if the transport is closed
   736	        // on entry, return Err(Closed) immediately, regardless of
   737	        // whether the local progress counter has already caught up.
   738	        // The prior impl returned Ok in this case, contradicting the
   739	        // docstring + the closed-on-entry test which now accepts
   740	        // both Ok and Err. After this closure, the contract is "Err
   741	        // if closed at any point — entry or mid-wait".
   742	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   743	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   744	        }
   745	        let target = self.queued_count.load(Ordering::SeqCst);
   746	        let (counter_lock, cv) = &*self.progress;
   747	        let mut counter = counter_lock
   748	            .lock()
   749	            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("flush_pending lock poisoned: {e}")))?;
   750	        while *counter < target {
   751	            if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   752	                return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   753	            }
   754	            let (new_counter, _timeout_result) = cv
   755	                .wait_timeout(counter, Duration::from_millis(100))
   756	                .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("flush_pending wait poisoned: {e}")))?;
   757	            counter = new_counter;
   758	        }
   759	        Ok(())
   760	    }
   761	}

   763	// Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 4 — pin the Send invariant. CollabSession
   764	// stores `Box<dyn Transport + Send>`; if a future refactor adds a
   765	// non-Send field this stops compiling.
   766	const _ASSERT_WEBSOCKET_TRANSPORT_SEND: fn() = || {
   767	    fn assert_send<T: Send>() {}
   768	    assert_send::<WebSocketTransport>();
   769	};

   771	// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 3 (2026-05-21) — Tier J3.** Pin the

   775	// the type is actually Sync — `try_recv`'s `&mut self` requirement
   776	// is enforced by the borrow checker, not by `!Sync`. All fields
   777	// happen to be Sync (mpsc handles, Arc<AtomicBool>, Arc<Mutex>,
   778	// JoinHandle). This positive assert pins the actual contract; if a
   779	// future refactor adds a non-Sync field (e.g., a Cell<>), it stops
   780	// compiling — same protection level as the Send assert above.

   102	/// Errors emitted by `Transport` impls.
   103	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
   104	#[non_exhaustive]
   105	pub enum TransportError {

   122	/// Wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
   123	///
   124	/// Implementations push and pull opaque byte blobs. The blobs ARE
   125	/// Loro `LoroDoc::export(...)` snapshots / updates; the
   126	/// transport doesn't interpret them. Framing (where one blob ends

   128	pub trait Transport {

   134	    /// 2026-05-21):** for buffered impls (`WebSocketTransport` and

   136	    /// returned Ok" and "bytes on the wire" where the bytes can be
   137	    /// silently lost (transport dropped, peer disconnect mid-flush,
   138	    /// allocator failure during the writer task). The session's
   139	    /// [`crate::CollabSession::flush_delta_to_transport`] advances

   147	    /// ack. For stronger guarantees (peer-application ack, TCP-level
   148	    /// ack), keep the transport attached until you've verified peer
   149	    /// reception out-of-band (e.g., via a custom ack-op layered on
   150	    /// `merge_bytes`). On transport drop or `Err(Closed)`, detach +

   152	    /// contract re-sends from empty VV.
   153	    ///
   154	    /// On error, the caller should treat the byte blob as unsent

   156	    /// transport will not retry automatically — that policy lives

   161	    /// Non-blocking receive. Returns `Some(bytes)` if a blob is

   201	    /// Block until every byte blob previously queued via `send` has
   202	    /// been written to the underlying transport's wire (e.g. WebSocket
   203	    /// sink). Default `Ok(())` for synchronous transports

   238	    fn flush_pending(&mut self) -> Result<(), TransportError> {

   241	}

    49	#[cfg(not(feature = "noop"))]
    50	static USER_DEFINED_RT: OnceLock<RwLock<Option<Runtime>>> = OnceLock::new();
